한국어

NRIC, 원자력 에너지 발사대 신규 참가자 13개 프로젝트 선정

원본: https://inl.gov/news-release/nric-selects-new-nuclear-energy-launch-pad-participants-to-boost-nuclear-energy/

NRIC, 원자력 에너지 발사대 신규 참가자 13개 프로젝트 선정

2026년 8월 24일

요약

  • 카테고리: 원자력 정책
  • 미국 에너지부 산하 국가원자로혁신센터(NRIC)가 아이다호 국립연구소에서 핵에너지 론치패드 이니셔티브에 참여할 12개 기업, 13개 신규 프로젝트를 선정했다.
  • 선정된 프로젝트는 원자로 개발과 핵연료 주기 발전(제조, 농축, 전환 기술 포함)을 포함하며, Oklo, Lightbridge Corporation, Valar Atomics 등 다양한 기업이 참여한다.
  • 론치패드는 INL 부지 내 Launch Pad INL과 전국 연방·비연방 부지를 대상으로 한 Launch Pad USA 두 가지 경로를 제공하며, 민간 첨단 원자력 기술의 신속한 상용화를 지원한다.

뉴스 보도

NRIC, 원자력 에너지 부흥을 위한 새로운 원자력 에너지 런치패드 참가자 선정

2026년 8월 24일

INL 미디어 관계팀

(아이다호주 아이다호 폴스) –– 오늘, 아이다호 국립연구소(Idaho National Laboratory)에 위치한 미국 에너지부 산하 국가원자로혁신센터(NRIC, National Reactor Innovation Center)는 원자력 에너지 런치패드(Nuclear Energy Launch Pad) 이니셔티브에 참가할 13개의 새로운 프로젝트를 선정했다고 발표했습니다.

NRIC 소장 브래드 토머(Brad Tomer)는 "이번 선정은 자신들의 기술을 발전시킬 준비가 된 개발자들로부터 강하고 성장하는 관심을 보여줍니다"라고 말했습니다. "원자력 에너지 런치패드는 이 개발자들에게 DOE 승인 절차로 나아가는 우선적 경로를 제공하고, 각자의 필요에 맞춘 분야별 전문가, 시설, 규제 지원에 대한 접근권을 제공합니다. 우리는 개념과 상용화 사이의 격차를 좁히는 데 힘쓰고 있으며, 이번 선정은 그 파이프라인이 더욱 강해지고 있음을 보여줍니다."

DOE와 NRIC는 이니셔티브의 응용 프로그램 요청서에 공개된 다양한 기준에 따라 12개 개발사와 13개 프로젝트를 선정했습니다. 선정된 프로젝트에는 원자로 개발과 제조, 농축, 전환 기술을 포함한 원자력 연료 주기 발전이 포함됩니다. 선정된 기업들은 런치패드 이니셔티브를 통해 제공되는 강화된 기술, 규제, 배치 지원을 실행하기 위해 NRIC와 협력을 시작할 수 있습니다.

오늘의 선정 기업은 다음과 같습니다:

안타레스 뉴클리어(Antares Nuclear) 아틀라스 아토믹스(Atlas Atomics) 디플로이어블 에너지(Deployable Energy) (2개 프로젝트) 포지 아토믹스(Forge Atomics) 헥시움 주식회사(Hexium Inc.) 라이트브리지 코퍼레이션(Lightbridge Corporation) 누사노 주식회사(Nusano, Inc.) 오클로(Oklo) 레이븐-플린트 뉴클리어(Raven-Flint Nuclear) 스케일드 아토믹스(Scaled Atomics) 서블라임 뉴클리어(Sublime Nuclear) 발라르 아토믹스(Valar Atomics)

NRIC는 런치패드 신청을 계속 받을 예정이며, 자원이 허용하는 한 순차적으로 추가 선정이 이루어질 것입니다. 이번 검토 주기에서 선정되지 않은 프로젝트는 제출 내용을 수정하여 향후 라운드에 재신청할 수 있습니다.

원자력 연료 주기 담당 차관보 대리 조시 재럴(Josh Jarrell)은 "탄력적인 원자력 연료 공급망은 미국의 에너지 미래에 필수적이며, 오늘 선정된 프로젝트들은 제조, 농축, 전환 기술에서의 실질적인 진전을 반영합니다"라고 말했습니다. "DOE는 국내 연료 공급 강화와 외국 의존도 감소를 위해 이 기업들과 함께 협력하는 데 최선을 다하고 있습니다." 원자로 담당 차관보 대행 치미 자콧(Chimi Zacot)은 "DOE의 원자로 파일럿 프로그램과 연료 라인 파일럿 프로그램의 초기 모멘텀을 바탕으로, 이번 선정은 그러한 노력들이 일회성 성공이 아님을 증명합니다"라고 말했습니다. "우리가 목격하는 진전은 배치를 향해 나아가는 지속적인 첨단 원자력 프로젝트 파이프라인의 시작입니다." 아이다호 국립연구소 소장 존 와그너(John Wagner)는 "아이다호 국립연구소의 가장 큰 강점 중 하나는 국가의 원자력 임무를 지원하기 위해 구축한 세계 수준의 인프라, 기술 전문성, 수십 년의 운영 경험의 조합입니다"라고 말했습니다. "원자력 에너지 런치패드 이니셔티브는 이러한 역량을 개발자들에게 제공하여 안전하고 효율적으로 기술을 설계에서 배치로 전환할 수 있도록 돕습니다."

#### 원자력 에너지 런치패드 소개

원자력 에너지 런치패드 이니셔티브는 민간 산업계에 의한 첨단 원자력 기술의 신속한 개발 및 구현을 촉진합니다. 이 이니셔티브는 2025년 6월에 수립된 DOE의 원자로 파일럿 프로그램과 2025년 8월에 시작된 연료 라인 파일럿 프로그램의 성공을 기반으로 합니다. 런치패드는 연방 및 비연방 부지 모두에서 유연한 기술적·규제적 프레임워크를 활용하여 첨단 원자력 기술 배치를 가속화합니다.

런치패드는 원자력 개발자들에게 두 가지 경로를 제공합니다: INL에 위치한 런치패드 INL은 세계 수준의 원자력 연구 인프라, 수십 년의 운영 전문성, 간소화된 규제 지원에 대한 접근을 제공하며, 런치패드 USA는 프로그램의 유연한 프레임워크를 전국의 연방 및 비연방 부지로 확장합니다. 이러한 경로들은 개발자들이 있는 곳에서 만나 미국의 에너지 수요가 요구하는 속도로 개념에서 배치로 나아갈 수 있도록 돕습니다.

런치패드는 NRIC에 의해 관리되며, NRIC는 첨단 원자력 기술의 개념, 실증, 상용화 사이의 격차를 해소하기 위해 산업계 및 국립연구소와 협력하기 위해 2019년에 설립되었습니다.

언론 미디어 연락처:

졸린 한센(Joelyn Hansen), 208-201-7650, joelyn.hansen@inl.gov 사라 노이만(Sarah Neumann), 208-520-1651, sarah.neumann@inl.gov

#### 아이다호 국립연구소 소개

배텔 에너지 얼라이언스(Battelle Energy Alliance)는 미국 에너지부(DOE) 원자력에너지국을 위해 아이다호 국립연구소(INL)를 운영합니다. INL은 원자력 에너지 연구개발의 국가 중심 기관으로, 에너지, 국가 안보, 과학, 환경 등 DOE의 각 전략적 목표 분야에서도 연구를 수행하고 있습니다. 더 많은 정보는 www.inl.gov를 방문하세요.

소셜 미디어에서 팔로우하세요:

AI 분석

미국 에너지부 산하 국가원자로혁신센터(NRIC)가 '원자력 에너지 런치패드(Nuclear Energy Launch Pad)' 이니셔티브를 통해 12개 기업의 13개 프로젝트를 신규 선정했으며, 이는 Oklo, Lightbridge 등 다양한 첨단 원자력 스타트업을 포함한다. 이번 선정은 2025년 6월 출범한 DOE의 원자로 파일럿 프로그램과 연료라인 파일럿 프로그램을 기반으로, 원자로 개발뿐 아니라 핵연료 가공·농축·전환 등 핵연료 주기 전반을 아우르는 광범위한 지원을 제공한다는 점에서 의미가 크다. 이 이니셔티브는 민간 개발자들이 아이다호 국립연구소(INL)의 세계적 수준의 인프라·전문 인력·규제 지원에 접근할 수 있는 명확한 경로를 제공함으로써, 개념 설계에서 실제 배치까지의 속도를 크게 높이려는 정책적 의지를 반영한다. 특히 국내 핵연료 공급망 강화와 외국 의존도 감소를 명시적 목표로 제시함으로써, 에너지 안보 차원에서도 전략적 중요성을 지닌다. 이번 선정이 롤링 방식으로 지속적으로 이루어질 예정임을 감안할 때, 미국의 첨단 원자력 기술 상용화 파이프라인은 앞으로 더욱 확대될 전망이다.

※ 이 글은 해외 원자력 바로알기를 위한 정보 전달을 목적으로 제공됩니다. 특정 기업이나 종목에 대한 투자 권유가 아니며, 모든 투자 판단과 그에 따른 책임은 투자자 본인에게 있습니다.

한국어

미국, 중국의 조선업 지배에 맞서 원자력 추진 상선 함대 계획

AI 분석

이 기사는 미국이 원자력 추진 상선 함대 구축을 통해 중국의 글로벌 조선업 지배에 대응하려는 전략적 움직임을 다루고 있다. 원자력 추진 상선은 연료 보급 없이 장거리 항해가 가능해 상업적·군사적 측면에서 전략적 우위를 제공할 수 있으며, 이는 미·중 간 해양 패권 경쟁의 새로운 국면을 열 수 있다. 중국은 현재 전 세계 신조 선박의 약 50% 이상을 건조하는 조선 강국으로, 미국의 이번 계획은 이에 대한 직접적인 도전으로 해석된다. 원자력 추진 기술의 민간 상선 적용은 기술적·규제적·경제적 과제를 수반하지만, 에너지 안보와 전략적 자율성 강화 측면에서 중요한 정책 방향으로 주목받고 있다. 다만 이 기사는 유료 장벽으로 인해 세부 내용을 확인할 수 없어, 구체적인 정책 내용이나 업계 반응에 대한 분석은 제한적이다.

한국어

원자로가 병목이 아니다 — 단조 공장이 병목이다

원본: https://neutronbytes.com/2026/08/24/the-reactor-isnt-the-bottleneck-the-forge-is/

원자로가 병목이 아니다 — 단조 공장이 병목이다

2026-08-24

요약

  • 카테고리: 기술
  • 선진 원자로 건설의 핵심 제약은 원자로 설계나 물리학이 아닌, 원자로 압력 용기(RPV)의 대형 단조 생산 능력으로 이동했다.
  • 미국은 1970년대 이후 대규모 단조 능력을 상실했으며, 현재 북미에는 현대 대형 원자로에 필요한 규모의 단조 프레스가 존재하지 않는다.
  • 중국은 전략적 자원으로 중공업 단조 설비에 지속 투자해 왔으며, 5개 기업이 세계 최대 규모의 Gen III+ 단조 작업을 수행할 수 있는 역량을 보유하고 있다.
게스트 블로그 포스트 작성: 엘리엇 마쉬(Elliot Marsh), NeutronRise 뉴트론 바이츠(Neutron Bytes)는 최근 원자력 르네상스의 현황에 관한 나침반 해설에서 이러한 변화를 조명했습니다. 선진 원자로, 소형 모듈 원자로, 마이크로 원자로의 핵심 제약이 상류 공정인 제조 부문으로 이동했으며, 더 이상 원자로 물리학에 있지 않습니다. 설계는 대부분 완성되었습니다. 이제 발전소가 일정대로 건설될 수 있는지를 결정하는 요소는 원자로 핵심부에 사용되는 중형 강재가 단조, 인증, 적시 현장 납품이 가능한지 여부입니다. 이 글은 고수준 저농축 우라늄(HALEU) 이송에 관한 앞선 글에 이어지는 내용입니다. 동일한 실행 격차를 두 번째로 살펴보는 이 글은 원자력 발전소 건설에서 가장 까다로운 단일 부품인 원자로 압력 용기에서 시작합니다. 로사톰(Rosatom)이 건설 중인 헝가리 1,200MW 팍스 II(Paks II) VVER의 원자로 압력 용기 단조 작업 이미지: World Nuclear News

압력 용기의 납품 시기가 공정 속도를 결정하는 이유

원자로 압력 용기는 원자로 노심을 감싸는 강철 외각입니다. 제조사들은 이를 여러 작은 부품을 용접하는 방식보다 소수의 대형 일체형 링으로 단조하는 방식을 선호합니다. 그 이유는 최대 80년에 달할 수 있는 발전소의 운전 수명과 관련이 있습니다.

용기 내의 모든 용접부는 발전소 운전 기간 동안 정기적인 일정에 따라 검사를 받아야 합니다. 용접부의 수가 발전소가 운전 수명 동안 수행해야 하는 검사 작업의 빈도를 결정합니다. 용접부가 적을수록 수십 년 후 결함이 발생할 수 있는 부위가 줄어듭니다. 이것이 바로 용접 공장이 아닌 단조 프레스의 규모와 생산 능력이 이러한 용기를 얼마나 빨리 생산할 수 있는지의 속도를 결정하는 이유입니다.

물리적 요건은 두 가지로 나뉩니다.

 첫째, 세계원자력협회(World Nuclear Association)는 초대형 3세대 플러스(Generation III-plus) 원자로의 단조 능력을 약 14,000~15,000톤 규모의 프레스로 제시합니다. 둘째, 해당 프레스는 500~600톤의 뜨거운 강철 잉곳을 처리할 수 있어야 합니다. 이 규모의 프레스는 희귀합니다. 단일 단조 공장은 다른 작업과의 일정을 조율하면서 연간 약 4기의 원자로 용기를 생산합니다. 원자로의 압력 경계는 물리학적 문제가 되기 훨씬 전에 야금학 및 기계 가공의 문제입니다.

미국은 이 능력을 포기했다

문제는 국내 생산 격차에서 시작됩니다. 이것이 핵심 쟁점입니다. 미국은 과거에 이 규모의 강철을 단조할 수 있었습니다. 1970년대에는 두 개의 미국 제철소에서 300톤 잉곳을 처리하는 8,000톤 프레스를 가동했으며, 이는 당시 원자로에 적합한 규모였습니다. 그러나 주문이 끊기자 아무도 재투자를 하지 않았고, 그 능력은 사라졌습니다.

오늘날 북미에는 현대 대형 원자로가 필요로 하는 프레스 규모에 근접한 것이 없습니다. 유럽에서 가장 큰 단조 공장은 최대 12,000톤을 처리할 수 있습니다. 일본제강소(Japan Steel Works)는 14,000톤을, 한국의 두산(Doosan)은 최대 17,000톤을 처리할 수 있습니다. 다른 지역 단조 공장의 최대 잉곳 크기는 프레스 능력과 함께 제한 요소가 됩니다. _아래 표 2 참조._

이제 누가 계속 원자로를 건설해 왔는지 살펴보겠습니다. 중국은 중형 단조를 전략적 자원으로 간주하고 침체기에도 지속적으로 투자했습니다. 중국제일중공업(China First Heavy Industries)은 대형 단조를 수행할 수 있는 중국의 5개 중공업 기업 중 하나(_아래 표 참조_)이며, 가장 큰 3세대 플러스 단조물을 만들 수 있는 전 세계 소수의 기업 중 하나입니다. _아래 표 1 참조._

표 1: 중국의 단조 공장. 데이터: 세계원자력협회(World Nuclear Association)

중국제일중공업(China First Heavy)은 중국의 국내 원자로 설계인 화룡 원(Hualong One)과 중국의 신형 대형 및 소형 모듈 설계를 위한 최초의 압력 용기를 생산했습니다. 화룡 설계는 이후 1,217메가와트 용량의 업그레이드된 2.0 버전으로 발전했습니다.

2026년 7월 말, 중국 일반 원자력(CGN)에 따르면 국무원은 저장성, 광둥성, 랴오닝성, 산둥성에 걸쳐 8기의 추가 원전을 승인했으며, 그 중 4기는 화룽 원(Hualong One) 2.0 실증 원자로입니다. 이 같은 건설 속도는 중국이 유지하기로 선택한 국내 제조 기반에 의존하고 있습니다. 또한 이는 성숙한 공급망과 숙련된 인력을 반영합니다.

서방 프로젝트의 경우 현실적인 공급업체 목록은 짧으며, 단순히 기계 설비 이상의 요인에 의해 형성됩니다. 일본의 재팬 스틸 웍스(Japan Steel Works)와 한국의 두산 에너빌리티(Doosan Enerbility)가 이 목록의 주축을 이루며, 체코 기업 스코다 JS(Skoda JS)가 마무리 및 양산 전 작업을 담당합니다.

상업적 자격 인증, 수출 통제, 그리고 기존 벤더 관계 모두가 이용 가능한 단조 업체 목록을 좁히고 있습니다. 제약은 대안이 없는 단일 해외 설비에 있지 않습니다. 문제는 신규 원자로에 필요한 필수 장기 조달 부품을 공급할 수 있는, 자격을 갖춘 공급업체의 생태계가 소규모라는 점이며, 미국은 현재 그 목록에 포함된 기업이 없습니다.

대형 원자로 압력 용기 제작이 가능한 대형 단조 프레스 --- 공급업체| 국가| 최대 프레스 용량| 최대 잉곳| 현황 두산 에너빌리티(Doosan Enerbility)| 한국| 17,000톤| 540톤| 운용 중, WNA 표 기준 최대 규모 재팬 스틸 웍스(Japan Steel Works)| 일본| 14,000톤| 600톤| 운용 중, 오랫동안 기본 공급업체 중국제일중형기계(China First Heavy Industries)| 중국| 15,000톤| 500~600톤| 운용 중, 중국 국내 건설 수요 담당 미국, 1970년대| 미국| 8,000톤| 300톤| 역량 퇴역 표 2: 세계원자력협회(World Nuclear Association) 데이터 미국 항목은 현재 대규모로는 더 이상 존재하지 않는 1970년대의 역량을 설명합니다.

소형 모듈 원자로(SMR)는 상황을 바꾸지만 문제를 완전히 없애지는 않습니다. SMR의 원자로 용기는 더 작으며, 하나의 거대한 잉곳 대신 링 단조품과 판재로 제작할 수 있는 경우도 있어 물리적으로 제작 가능한 공장의 범위가 넓어집니다. 그러나 제작 능력과 원자력 인증을 동시에 갖춘 공장의 풀은 여전히 작으며, 이는 SMR 개발사나 투자자에게 결코 안심할 수 있는 상황이 아닙니다.

상업적으로 능력 있는 단조 공장이 원자력 인증 단조 공장은 아니다

이 점은 냉혹한 현실 점검입니다. 어떤 공장이 거대한 프레스를 보유하고 있다 해도, 원자로 부품 단 하나도 제작하는 것이 허용되지 않을 수 있습니다. 기술적으로 작업이 충분히 가능하더라도, NRC 규정과 관련 ASME 품질 기준에 근거한 ASME NQA-1 인증 없이는 어떤 원자로 벤더도 해당 제품을 시설에 사용할 수 없습니다. 두 종류의 공급업체를 구분하는 것은 품질 보증이며, 이것이 일정이 조용히 무너지는 지점이기 때문에 평이한 용어로 설명할 필요가 있습니다.

신규 원자로에 대한 품질 보증 규정은 한 곳에 집중되어 있지 않습니다. 미국에서는 모든 원자로 개발을 허가하거나 중단시킬 수 있는 연방 기관인 원자력규제위원회(NRC)가 10 CFR 50 부록 B로 알려진 규정에 따라 18개 기준을 담은 의무 규칙을 발행합니다. 목록의 요건 자체가 특별히 난해한 것은 없습니다. 요컨대, 로켓 과학은 아니지만, 이를 이끄는 안전 문제로 인해 기준 준수는 신규 원자력 원자로 건설에 수반되는 가장 엄격한 과정 중 하나가 됩니다.

그럼에도 불구하고 18개 기준 각각은 문서화된 상식입니다. 마지막 기준인 감사(audit)는 나머지 기준을 실질적으로 만드는 항목으로, 규정이 실제로 준수되었는지를 검증하는 시험입니다. 감사는 부품을 구매하고 시설을 건설한 계약업체들이 단순히 약속한 것이 아니라 규정에 따라 실제로 작업을 수행했는지를 기관이 확인하는 방법입니다.

업계의 오래된 격언이 이 규정의 중요성을 잘 포착합니다. '요건이 느슨하거나 문서화되지 않은 곳에서는 품질과 결함이 모두 무작위로 나타난다.' 이 기관의 규정은 느슨함과는 거리가 멉니다. 규정은 세밀하게 규정된 요건이며, 이를 충족하려면 훈련된 인력, 관리된 장비, 그리고 매 단계마다 문서화된 증거가 필요합니다. 감사는 성과뿐 아니라 기록도 점검합니다. 이러한 역량은 비용이 많이 들고 구축하는 데 시간이 걸립니다. 이것이 바로 어떤 상업 고객에게도 서비스를 제공할 수 있는 단조 공장이 자동으로 원자로에도 서비스를 제공할 수 있는 공장이 되지 않는 이유입니다.

천둥은 소음에 불과하고, 번개가 일을 한다

독자들은 NRC가 규정을 발표하면 그 다음은 규정 준수를 달성하기 위한 작업이 따른다고 생각할 수 있습니다. 실제로는 NRC의 품질 보증 기준을 충족하기 위한 규정 준수는 2단계 과정입니다. 표준은 규정을 따릅니다. 원자력 규제 기관은 일반적으로 독립적인 표준 기관이 세부적인 방법을 작성하도록 의존한 다음, 자체 프레임워크에서 그 작업을 참조합니다.

캐나다는 원자력 발전소에 대한 품질 보증 프로그램을 이런 방식으로 운영합니다. 캐나다 표준 협회(Canadian Standards Association) (CSA)가 원자력 관리 및 품질 표준을 작성하고, 캐나다 원자력 안전 위원회(Canadian Nuclear Safety Commission, CNSC)는 이를 면허 신청 및 새로운 원자력 발전소의 실제 건설에서 요구하는 사항에 포함시킵니다.

미국은 규제 기관이 의존하는 코드를 보유한 기관인 미국 기계 공학 학회(American Society of Mechanical Engineers) (ASME)를 통해 동일한 방식으로 작동합니다. NQA-1로 알려진 품질 표준은 NRC의 기준과 거의 일대일로 매핑되며, 공급업체에게 부지 선정, 설계, 건설, 운영 및 폐로 측면에서 이를 구현하는 방법을 알려줍니다. 요컨대, 품질 보증 요건은 첫 번째 콘크리트 타설부터 현재 시대에는 100년에 달할 수 있는 서비스 수명 종료까지 원자력 발전소의 모든 단계에서 주어진 사항입니다.

원자로에 납품하는 공급업체는 하나가 아닌 두 가지 인증이라는 이중 혜택을 받습니다. 방금 설명한 품질 프로그램 표준이 있고, 원자력 부품을 제조할 권한이 있는 공장을 표시하는 스탬프인 ASME 섹션 III(ASME Section III)라는 원자력 부품에 대한 별도의 건설 코드도 있습니다. 어느 하나를 취득하는 것도 비용이 많이 들고 시간이 오래 걸리며, 이를 유지하려면 약 3년 주기로 지속적인 직원 교육과 반복 감사를 통과해야 합니다.

기관의 병행 관심사이자 감독 역할은 원자로나 발전소의 다른 장비에 위조 부품이 사용되는 것을 방지하는 것입니다. NQA-1 인증을 받지 않은 부품은 경고 없이 고장날 수 있기 때문에, 위조 부품은 최우선 안전 우려 사항입니다. NRC 규정 10CFR21 결함 및 미준수 보고(10CFR21 Reporting Of Defects And Noncompliance)에는 가짜 부품 및 장비를 식별하고 보고하는 데 필요한 모든 내용이 포함되어 있습니다. 다른 품질 보증 요건과 마찬가지로 준수는 의무 사항입니다.

대형 단조품과 마찬가지로, 더 넓은 시장에 서비스를 제공할 수 있는 공급업체가 반드시 원자력 자격을 갖춘 것은 아니며, 이러한 인증을 취득하고 유지하는 비용으로 인해 그 목록은 짧게 유지됩니다. 원자로 부품의 처리량 한계는 프레스 톤수만의 문제가 아닙니다. 동시에 규모에 맞게 단조하고 인증을 보유할 수 있는 공장의 수가 문제입니다.

펌프 공급량 늘리기

AP1000 원자로 냉각재 펌프. 이미지: 커티스-라이트(Curtis-Wright)

단조 공장이 가장 극명한 사례이지만, 유일한 사례는 아닙니다. 동일한 패턴이 발전소의 장납기 부품 전반에 걸쳐 반복되며, 원자로 냉각재 펌프(reactor coolant pumps) (RCP)에서 이를 명확히 볼 수 있습니다. 각 웨스팅하우스(Westinghouse) AP1000은 4개를 사용하므로 쌍으로 구성된 세트는 8개가 필요합니다. 보그틀(Vogtle) 부지의 쌍둥이 AP1000에 제작 및 납품된 RCP는 각각 187.5톤, 즉 375,000파운드에 달했습니다.

이것들은 캔드 모터 펌프이며, 미국에서는 1950년대 중반 시핑포트(Shippingport) 시대부터 원자로 부품을 제작해 온 펜실베이니아주 체스윅(Cheswick)에 있는 커티스-라이트(Curtiss-Wright)의 단일 공장에서 생산됩니다.

처리량 계산은 단조 계산과 매우 유사합니다. 최초 4기의 AP1000 원자로에 16개의 펌프를 공급하는 커티스-라이트(Curtiss-Wright)의 원래 계약은 2007년부터 2015년과 2016년까지 이어진 납품으로 진행되었습니다.

이는 수년에 걸쳐 측정되는 자격 취득 및 제작 주기입니다. 회사의 최근 투자자 관련 발언에 대한 업계 보도에 따르면 현재 생산 능력은 연간 약 12~16개의 펌프, 즉 약 3~4기의 원자로 분량으로, 단일 대형 프레스가 처리하는 연간 4기의 원자로와 같은 규모입니다. 이 제약은 천천히 해결되고 있습니다.

2026년 7월, 커티스-라이트(Curtiss-Wright)는 체스윅(Cheswick) 공장 확장에 8천만 달러를 투자하겠다고 약속했으며, 납기 단축을 목표 중 하나로 명시했습니다. 이 투자는 두 가지를 동시에 확인해 줍니다. 병목 현상은 실재하며, 이를 해소하는 것은 단기적 해결책이 아닌 수년에 걸친 구축 작업이라는 점입니다.

이 이미지는 격납 구조물 내 가압경수로(PWR)에서 두 개의 원자로 냉각재 펌프(RCP)의 위치를 보여줍니다.

상용 일정 가정이 일정 지연으로 이어질 수 있는 이유

여기서 타이밍 메커니즘이 실제 비용 문제로 전환됩니다. 공장 건조 원자로 모델, 특히 소형 모듈 원자로(SMR)는 인증된 부품이 일반적인 상업적 납기 내에 도착한다는 것을 전제로 합니다. 그러나 실제로는 그렇지 않습니다. 인증된 용기, 밸브, 또는 펌프의 납기가 지연되면 전체 건설 순서가 함께 지연되고, 인력은 대기 상태에 놓이며, 현장에서 아무런 생산적인 작업이 이루어지지 않는 동안 금융 비용이 누적됩니다. 이러한 장납기 품목들은 이를 사용하는 모든 유형의 원자로에서 핵심 경로(critical path)에 놓여 있습니다.

이러한 병목 병목현상이 수요에 맞춰 단순히 해소되지 않는 데에는 더 깊은 이유가 있습니다. 이러한 병목 현상을 분석한 업계 단체인 원자력 확장 이니셔티브(Nuclear Scaling Initiative)가 발표한 최근 공급망 보고서는 자기 강화적 함정을 설명합니다.

다음은 이 보고서에서 도출된 다섯 가지 핵심 사항으로, 모두 EPC(설계·조달·시공) 업체와 그 고객인 전력 회사들이 밤잠을 설칠 만한 문제들입니다.

 모든 기술에 대한 연료 공급은 차별화되어 있으나 상호 연결된 제약을 반영합니다. 제조 병목 현상은 후방 공정에 집중되어 있습니다. 노동력 부족은 건설 업종을 넘어 광범위하게 나타납니다. 핵심 소재의 변동성과 의존성은 전략적 리스크를 야기합니다. 규제 및 자격 인증 장벽이 규모 확장을 제한합니다.

이 보고서에 대한 심층 분석과 다양한 이해관계자들이 이러한 문제를 해결하기 위해 취해야 할 실질적인 권고 사항을 확인하시려면 아래의 보고서 관련 웨비나 영상을 시청하시기 바랍니다.

공급업체들은 확정 주문을 확인하기 전까지는 인증된 생산 능력을 늘리는 데 막대한 비용을 투자하려 하지 않으며, 구매자들은 생산 능력과 일정이 실질적으로 보장되지 않는 한 확정 주문을 내리려 하지 않습니다. 이는 연료 분야에서 이미 잘 알려진 교착 상태와 동일한 구조로, 기업들이 아직 가시화되지 않은 수요를 앞에 두고 새로운 농축 경제성의 비용을 저울질하는 상황과 같습니다. 아무도 먼저 움직이지 않으며, 대기 행렬은 저절로 줄어들지 않습니다.

이것은 전형적인 닭이 먼저냐 달걀이 먼저냐의 딜레마이며, 바로 이 때문에 미국 에너지부(DOE)가 이를 해결하기 위해 175억 달러를 투입하고 있습니다. DOE가 웨스팅하우스(Westinghouse)에 두 기씩 짝지어 건설해 주길 바라는 AP1000의 건설 여부 및 시기는 아직 미정입니다. 첫 번째 쌍둥이 원자로 세트에는 원자로당 4개, 즉 총 8개의 냉각재 펌프(RCP)가 필요합니다.

AP1000을 통한 원자력 르네상스를 향한 DOE의 비전은 전적으로 미국 공급망의 견고성 문제에 달려 있습니다. 4개의 추가 쌍둥이 세트에는 세트당 8개의 펌프가 필요하며, 총 32개의 냉각재 펌프가 요구됩니다. 만약 실현된다면 40개의 펌프가 필요한 총 10기의 AP1000 "함대" 전체의 건설 일정은 공급망 제약을 수용하기 위해 현실적인 단계로 단계적으로 추진되어야 할 것입니다.

경수로(LWR) 설계 원리에 기반한 소형 모듈 원자로(SMR)의 상황은 덜 명확합니다. 일부 소형 설계는 전체 냉각 및 증기 시스템을 단일 어셈블리에 통합합니다. 또 다른 300MW급 설계들은 원자로 압력 용기를 외부 펌프 및 외부 증기 시스템과 연결합니다.

헬륨, 이산화탄소, 또는 용융염을 이용해 원자로의 열을 터빈 구동에 활용하는 선진 원자로는 냉각재 펌프(RCP)를 사용하지 않습니다. 그러나 이러한 방식으로 증기 시스템 및 터빈에 열을 전달하는 장비를 제작하는 데 필요한 맞춤형 공구류에는 그 나름의 도전 과제들이 따릅니다.

교착 상태를 해소하고 닭을 더 많이 확보하기 위한 실효성 있는 방안

세 가지 핵심 방안이 닭이 먼저냐 달걀이 먼저냐의 딜레마에 내재된 제약 중 일부를 해소할 수 있으며, 이 중 어느 것도 문제를 완전히 없애지는 못하지만, 지연을 줄이고 건설 일정을 예측 가능한 속도로 앞당길 수 있습니다.

첫 번째 방안은 조기 구매와 병행 발주입니다. 가장 긴 대기열을 가진 부품들을 인허가 취득 이후에 발주하는 대신, 프로젝트는 인허가 절차가 진행 중인 동안 제조 슬롯을 미리 확보할 수 있습니다.

첫 번째이자 가장 명확한 조치는 이미 시행 중에 있다. 이는 2026년 6월, 에너지부(DOE)가 자금 조달 기관을 통해 최대 10기의 웨스팅하우스(Westinghouse) AP1000 원자로에 필요한 장기 조달 품목을 구매하기 위해 최대 175억 달러를 조건부로 지원하기로 결정한 것이다. 이 조치의 명시적 목표는 해당 원자로들의 납품 기간을 최대 3년까지 단축하는 것이다.

이 자금이 실제로 무엇을 하는지, 그리고 무엇을 하지 않는지를 정확히 짚어볼 필요가 있다. 이 자금은 조달의 자본 위험과 시간적 위험을 줄여준다. 그러나 그 자체만으로는 자격을 갖춘 프레스 설비를 추가하거나 새로운 공급업체를 인증하지는 않는다. 앞서 설명한 교착 상태를 해소하지도 않는다. 보글(Vogtle) 3호기의 원자로 용기는 360톤에 달했으며 그 자체가 장기 조달 품목이었다는 사실은, 이 자금 조달이 극복하려는 물리적 현실을 잘 일깨워준다.

두 번째 조치는 품질 보증 기준선을 명확히 긋는 것이다. 가장 무거운 품질 보증 부담은 법적으로 안전 관련 원자력 섬(nuclear island)에 적용되며, 전체 부지에 적용되지는 않는다. 이러한 엄격한 기준을 규제가 요구하는 구역 내에 유지하고, 플랜트 나머지 부분(balance of plant)은 표준 산업 관행에 따라 운영함으로써, 희소한 인증 역량을 모든 곳에 얇게 분산시키는 대신 실질적으로 중요한 곳에 집중시킬 수 있다.

세 번째 조치는 공급업체 자격 인증 속도를 높이는 것이다. DOE와 주계약자들은 비용 분담 정부 지원을 포함한 체계적인 프로그램을 운영함으로써, 새로운 제조사들을 자격 인증 풀에 편입시켜 유능한 제조업체가 원자력 자격을 취득하는 데 걸리는 수년의 시간을 단축할 수 있다. 이를 통해 품질 보증 기준을 낮추지 않으면서도 시간이 지남에 따라 공급망의 생산 역량을 넓혀갈 수 있다.

이는 더 어려운 절반으로 이어진다

부품은 단조(鍛造)될 수 있고 인증받을 수 있다. 더 어려운 문제는 누가 그것들을 연결하느냐이다. 원자로는 수천 개의 규제 대상 용접 및 연결부로 이루어지며, 각각은 규제 기관이 승인하기 전에 검사를 받고 법적으로 문서화되어야 한다. 이 작업은 인증된 용접사, 배관공, 검사관에 달려 있는데, 이들 역시 극심한 인력 부족 상태에 있다. 다음 주 2부에서는 실행 격차의 인적 측면을 다룰 예정이다.

AI 분석

이 기사는 원자력 르네상스의 핵심 병목이 더 이상 원자로 설계나 물리학이 아니라 원자로 압력용기 단조 제조 능력에 있다는 중요한 구조적 전환을 조명한다. 대형 원자로 압력용기 제작에는 14,000~15,000톤급 프레스와 500~600톤 강철 잉곳 처리 능력이 필요하지만, 미국은 1970년대 이후 이 역량을 포기했고 현재 북미에는 이를 충족할 수 있는 설비가 없는 실정이다. 반면 중국은 China First Heavy Industries를 포함해 5개 기업이 대형 단조 능력을 보유하며 이를 전략 자원으로 유지·투자해 왔고, 일본 JSW(14,000톤)와 한국 두산(17,000톤)이 서방 진영의 주요 공급원 역할을 하고 있다. 결국 원자력 르네상스의 실현 가능성은 원자로 설계의 우열이 아니라 공급망 최상단의 중공업 단조 능력을 누가 얼마나 빠르게 확보하느냐에 달려 있으며, 이는 에너지 안보와 제조업 자립도 측면에서 서방 국가들이 시급히 대응해야 할 전략적 과제임을 시사한다.

※ 이 글은 해외 원자력 바로알기를 위한 정보 전달을 목적으로 제공됩니다. 특정 기업이나 종목에 대한 투자 권유가 아니며, 모든 투자 판단과 그에 따른 책임은 투자자 본인에게 있습니다.

A closer look at the initial NLIC selections—Part 2-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-08-24/article-8334/a-closer-look-at-the-initial-nlic-selectionspart-2/

A closer look at the initial NLIC selections—Part 2

August 24, 2026

TL;DR

  • The Department of Energy selected five states (Idaho, Louisiana, Oklahoma, Tennessee, and Utah) for the Nuclear Lifecycle Innovation Campus program exploring fuel cycle development partnerships
  • Utah has emerged as a nuclear growth leader despite never hosting a commercial power plant, with Operation Gigawatt initiative attracting multiple new reactor and fuel cycle projects
  • Utah offers robust front-end (uranium mining and milling) and back-end (waste management) fuel cycle infrastructure, with a proposed 300-square-mile NLIC campus site in Tooele County
Waste Management

A closer look at the initial NLIC selections—Part 2

August 24, 2026, 3:13PMNuclear News

An aerial view of ORNL's East and Central Campus. The Oak Ridge, Tenn., area could host Nuclear Lifecycle Innovation Campus functions. (Photo: ORNL)

In January, the Department of Energy announced its new Nuclear Lifecycle Innovation Campus (NLIC) program, inviting states via a request for information to express their interest in hosting a facility supporting work from the front to the back end of the nuclear fuel cycle.

By April, 26 states had expressed interest in hosting such a facility. At the end of July, the DOE signed memorandums of understanding with five states—Idaho, Louisiana, Oklahoma, Tennessee, and Utah—to more closely explore the possibilities of state-federal partnerships. These MOUs are not firm commitments from either the federal or state governments. Time will tell which—if any—of the five states develop projects through the program. In the meantime, today, we are taking a close look at what Utah, Idaho, Tennessee can offer in terms of a preexisting nuclear sector that could support new fuel cycle developments.

The first article in this two-part series focused on the nuclear offerings of Oklahoma and Louisiana.

Utah: Along with every other state selected by the DOE for a possible life cycle campus, Utah is a Nuclear Regulatory Commission Agreement State. However, it is also one of the 14 U.S. states that has never been home to a commercial nuclear power plant. It and Oklahoma are the only two states of the five to share this trait.

Despite this, Utah is associated with two canceled nuclear power projects. The first was the Blue Castle project, which aimed to bring two Westinghouse AP1000s to the city of Green River. Momentum for that project largely died out by the mid-2010s. (Earlier this summer, Hi Tech Solutions began work to revive the project, this time with Holtec SMR-300s.)

The more recently canceled nuclear project in Utah's past is the Carbon Free Power Project, a collaboration between Utah Associated Municipal Power Systems and NuScale Power. The project was to be sited at Idaho National Laboratory and would have provided power to the INL campus and customers in Utah and surrounding states. In 2015, the partners announced plans to develop 12 50-MWe NuScale reactors. Despite significant regulatory progress and financial investment, the plan—which changed in 2021 to six 77-MWe reactors—was ultimately canceled in 2023.

This shaky history stands in contrast to the state's new nuclear stance. In 2024, Utah Gov. Spencer Cox unveiled Operation Gigawatt, an initiative to double the state's power production by 2034, in part by promoting new nuclear development. Since then, a host of organizations have looked to develop projects in the state, including Valar Atomics, Holtec, Nuclea Energy, EnergySolutions, Anfield Energy, Utah State University, Elemental Nuclear, Nusano, the Military Installation Development Authority, TerraPower, and still more. Several of these projects were discussed in a recent American Nuclear Society webinar.

Utah also has a robust presence in the front end of the domestic fuel cycle. It has been home to numerous uranium mines and mills since the earliest days of the nuclear sector. Today, Energy Fuels runs the White Mesa mill in southern Utah—the only currently operating conventional uranium mill in the United States. (There are several operating _in situ_ recovery uranium facilities.) At the back end of the fuel cycle, EnergySolutions runs the Clive site, which manages low-level radioactive waste.

The state's Office of Energy Development (OED) has released both its full, 151-page response to the DOE's RFI and a high-level, 32-slide presentation summarizing the key elements of its plan. That plan proposes a single site for Utah's campus: a 300-square-mile corridor of state-controlled land in Tooele County. This site, which the OED highlights for its valuable road, air, rail, internet, and electricity connections, is planned to include enrichment, fuel fabrication, recycling and reprocessing, and long-term waste disposition. The state also envisions this campus as "supporting complementary capabilities such as advanced reactor deployment, advanced manufacturing, and a data center."

Idaho: Comprehensive summaries of the nuclear histories and current sectors of the remaining two finalists for the NLIC program are outside the scope of a single article. The current makeup and historical background of Idaho's and Tennessee's nuclear sectors, if told in full, would span hundreds of pages, with their beginnings stretching all the way back to World War II.

Earlier this year, when _Nuclear News_ ranked the top 10 states of nuclear, Idaho came in at number three. At the time _NN_ defended that ranking by pointing to—above all else—the massive institutional presence of Idaho National Laboratory. In the early days of nuclear, INL was the site where the first usable electricity was produced from nuclear power. In all, more than 50 reactors have come on line at the site, each of which helped lay the groundwork for our current understanding and development of nuclear power domestically and internationally. Today, organizations and facilities within INL like the National Reactor Innovation Center, the Demonstration of Microreactor Experiments test bed, and the Hot Fuel Examination Facility (to name a few) continue to push the envelope of the industry forward.

What's more, INL has also been the epicenter of one of the most-watched nuclear stories over the past year: the Reactor Pilot Program and its closely related Launch Pad program. Within and beyond those programs, INL works with dozens of organizations in private industry and education.

In the state's 113-page response to the DOE's RFI, Idaho Gov. Brad Little naturally starts by espousing the many benefits INL would bring to an NLIC campus, but he also points out that the state has significant supply chain, community engagement, and regulatory tailwinds that would boost the project.

As most readers would likely expect, Idaho's response confirms that the state plans to site its life cycle campus within the 890-square-mile INL site, arguing that its "geographic footprint, nuclear workforce, and existing site infrastructure" make it a natural fit.

While Idaho is open to siting new enrichment, fabrication, and reprocessing capacity at the INL site, its language regarding a long-term repository is notably less definitive than some of its competitors. The response reads that "Idaho is poised to engage in discussions about the eventual siting of a permanent national repository."

Later on, it clarifies its position slightly, saying that while the state is committed to research across the nuclear life cycle, it is essential that "permanent solutions" not be borne indefinitely by the state's communities, concluding that "the development of a permanent nuclear waste repository in neighboring Utah could provide a viable long-term solution for used fuel, helping to alleviate concerns in Idaho."

While this vision of its campus appears at odds with the DOE's aim to have an all-in-one, disposal-focused facility, Idaho's selection as one of the five finalists indicates that the DOE did not perceive its resistance to a permanent repository as a deal-breaker.

Tennessee: The complexity of Idaho's nuclear sector and history is perhaps only rivaled (and arguably superseded) by that of Tennessee. Returning to _NN_ 's top 10 states of nuclear, Tennessee came in at number one: the epicenter of new industry growth.

Like Idaho, Tennessee is home to one of the most important institutions in the nuclear community: Oak Ridge National Laboratory. ORNL's reputation is built on a storied history beginning as the key uranium enrichment site during the Manhattan Project. It was also the site of the third nuclear reactor ever built: the X-10. Like INL, its legacy carries into the present day; ORNL is now home to a complex network of research activities, test beds, and private partnerships exploring a broad scope of pure and applied science, including nuclear science and technology.

Looking beyond the labs, Tennessee has a much broader nuclear sector than any of the other four finalists. It is home to four commercial power reactors and has the eighth highest nuclear capacity of any U.S. state.

Those plants are managed by the Tennessee Valley Authority, the largest U.S. public energy provider. TVA is currently pursuing several advanced nuclear projects involving GE Vernova Hitchai, Kairos, Type One Energy, and NuScale. Those projects are complemented by significant fuel cycle projects in various stages of development from Centrus, Standard Nuclear, TRISO-X, Orano USA, Oklo, and LIS Technologies. Other significant nuclear institutions in the state include the Y-12 National Security Complex and the University of Tennessee.

Tennessee has published both a one-page summary of its RFI response and its full 207-page response. Its envisioned campus embraces the full fuel cycle, from enrichment to disposition.

Unlike Idaho, Tennessee clearly indicates that it is in the early stages of site assessment for a long-term geologic repository, though the response does acknowledge that, if another state is more suitable for said repository, "then Tennessee is open to exploring a collaborative solution."

The response further states that Tennessee aims to "transform the nuclear life cycle" by "diverting critical materials and isotopes into other health, defense, and industrial uses," which would substantially reduce "the country's needs for interim and long-term materials management solutions."

As for siting, like Idaho with INL, Tennessee intends for Oak Ridge to be the primary site for its innovation campus. The state expects that the city "would host most of the expected campus functions." However, the location of its potential repository is not definitively tied to Oak Ridge.

AI Analysis

The article highlights Utah's transformation into a nuclear innovation hub through the DOE's Nuclear Lifecycle Innovation Campus program, despite having no commercial nuclear power plants and a history of failed projects like the Carbon Free Power Project. Governor Spencer Cox's 2024 "Operation Gigawatt" initiative to double power production by 2034 has catalyzed unprecedented interest, with major players including Valar Atomics, Holtec, TerraPower, and EnergySolutions actively pursuing development projects in the state. This represents a significant strategic shift toward advanced reactor technologies and small modular reactors, positioning Utah as a key player in the nation's emerging nuclear fuel cycle ecosystem. The convergence of state policy support, private sector investment, and federal partnership through NLIC creates substantial momentum for establishing a meaningful nuclear industrial base in a state previously without commercial nuclear infrastructure.
English

Commonwealth Fusion Systems-Utility Dive

Source: https://www.utilitydive.com/news/what-a-billion-dollar-funding-means-for-commonwealth-fusion-systems-and-its/828515/

Commonwealth Fusion Systems aims to complete demonstration reactor with $1B funding round

Aug. 24, 2026

TL;DR

  • Commonwealth Fusion Systems raised $1 billion in new funding, bringing total to $4 billion, with major investors including pension funds, sovereign wealth funds, and industrial corporations (ENI and Google disclosed)
  • The company aims to complete its SPARC demonstration reactor (80% complete) and expects to achieve net energy gain in 2027, supporting its goal to deploy a 400-MW commercial plant in Virginia in the early 2030s
  • Commonwealth has secured permits, signed offtake agreements with Google and ENI, and partnered with Dominion Energy, demonstrating growing institutional confidence in commercial fusion viability despite expert debate on commercialization timelines

An article from

Commonwealth Fusion Systems aims to complete demonstration reactor with $1B funding round

CEO Bob Mumgaard said the reactor, SPARC, is now "about 80% complete." Experts remain divided over how soon they believe fusion technology could be commercialized.

Published Aug. 24, 2026

Herman K. Trabish Contributing Editor

Commonwealth Fusion Systems is building a fusion reactor in Devens, Mass. Permission granted by Commonwealth Fusion Systems

Listen to the article 9 min

This audio is auto-generated. Please let us know if you have feedback.

Devens, Massachusetts-based Commonwealth Fusion Systems has now raised $4 billion in its quest to build the first commercial fusion power plant anywhere in the world, following a fresh infusion of $1 billion that company executives say shows growing interest from risk-averse institutional investors.

Though only ENI and Google allowed their identities to be disclosed, the investors include pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and infrastructure and industrial corporations, said Bob Mumgaard, Commonwealth CEO and co-founder, according to the company's transcript of the press conference following the July 30 announcement of the funding.

Mumgaard said the money will allow Commonwealth to complete its demonstration reactor and put it on the path toward deploying its first commercial reactor.

"At a time when we need more energy for electrifying everything, fusion is emerging as a next big thing in energy," Mumgaard told reporters.

Fusion has the potential, like nuclear fission, to produce large amounts of firm power, but with far less hazardous radioactive waste.

After decades of publicly funded research, in 2022, scientists at the Lawrence Livermore National Laboratory in California made a vital breakthrough by achieving fusion ignition for the first time. The lab's director, Kim Budil, said at the time that commercial fusion was "probably decades" away.

Work has continued since, and fusion startups like Commonwealth are pushing much more aggressive timelines. But experts remain divided over how soon they believe the technology could be commercialized.

Commonwealth, which spun out of the Massachusetts Institute of Technology in 2018, has emerged as a leading player in the fusion space. The company is working toward building a 400-MW plant on land owned by Dominion Energy in Chesterfield County, Virginia – the Fall Line Fusion Power Station – which it aims to bring online in the early 2030s.

Commonwealth has secured some of the permits it needs for the project and has signed offtake agreements with Google and ENI, Mumgaard said, adding that it has also submitted an application to connect to the PJM Interconnection.

'Energy, density and time'

Commonwealth's technology is magnetic confinement fusion, said Electric Power Research Institute Senior Technical Executive Andrew Sowder.

The sun's energy is released by fusion of protons driven together by its gravity. Commonwealth's donut-shaped superconducting magnets, called a tokamak, similarly hold superheated plasma containing deuterium and tritium that, despite their like charges driving them apart, collide and release neutrons, Sowder said. At a high enough temperature, if the density is maintained long enough, the collision releases energy.

While LLNL's 2022 ignition was a notable achievement, it was only "scientific" net energy gain because it did not produce enough energy to power the entire fusion process.

In April, the national lab announced it would partner with fusion startup Inertia Enterprises to "inform the industrial-scale development that commercial fusion demands."

Sowder says scientists and the private sector are working toward meaningful net energy gain, but he declined to speculate about when fusion will achieve it.

"Energy, density, and time are the three variables fusion scientists can adjust," Sowder said in an interview.

Commonwealth's latest cash infusion will allow the company to complete its demonstration reactor, SPARC, which Mumgaard said is now "about 80% complete." He said the company expects to show net energy gain in 2027.

The company plans to take the lessons learned from SPARC and apply them to its next-generation reactor, ARC, which will power its flagship commercial plant in Virginia.

"This funding's commitments from infrastructure investors shows they could soon be interested in a string of ARC power plants," Mumgaard said.

That would take more than $1 billion, though not as much as $10 billion, which is "the scale of capital needed to do fusion seriously," he added.

Commonwealth's SPARC

Permission granted by Commonwealth Fusion

The utility and the offtake agreements

Dominion has said that in addition to leasing the company the land for the proposed power plant, it is providing non-financial collaboration, including development and technical expertise.

The utility did not respond to Utility Dive's request for further comment.

Commonwealth company officials told Utility Dive that they were initially unsure whether they would find a utility partner for the commercial reactor because utilities tend to be conservative about unproven technologies. They explored multiple states and regions where there is a demand for low-carbon, firm power, and for each location, it engaged with the incumbent utility to understand the feasibility of interconnecting a new generation source, they said.

The turning point came when Dominion's executive team visited the SPARC plant in Massachusetts and saw "what is being built and being planned for Virginia," said Ally Yost, Commonwealth's senior vice president of corporate development. "They left with a different view of where fusion is going," and the confidence to enter a joint development agreement in 2024, she recalled.

Following that partnership, Commonwealth signed offtake agreements in 2025 with ENI and Google. Under those contracts, electricity generated by ARC will be sold into the PJM wholesale market through Dominion's system, while the offtakers will pay for and receive clean energy credits, according to Commonwealth Chief Commercial Officer Rick Needham. He declined to disclose further details of the terms or the credit accreditation process.

Dominion Chair, President and CEO Robert Blue has said ARC generation could help meet the utility's anticipated 6% annual demand growth. The company recently told investors during a second-quarter earnings presentation that it has 53.8 GW of large loads in "various stages" of contracting.

Dominion has been targeted for acquisition by NextEra Energy in a deal that would create the country's largest regulated utility company. The companies' executives have said they anticipate closing the deal by the end of 2027, pending regulatory approvals.

Inside the tokamak

Permission granted by Commonwealth Fusion

The competition

Currently, the field of potential competitors is small. Type One Energy and the less well-funded Helion are the only other U.S. companies with offtake agreements, according to the Fusion Industry Association 2026 report.

None of the startups have yet proven their technology can achieve ignition, let alone work at a commercial scale.

Type One is building a 400-MW project at the Tennessee Valley Authority's Bull Run Complex, a former coal plant, with TVA as the offtaker, according to the industry report and public statements by the utility.

Type One says its curving tokamak, called a "stellarator," may allow more stable reactions and lower energy consumption.

Helion Energy's 500-MW linear accelerator in Washington state will use magnets to drive two plasma sources toward one another to create fusion, its website reports. It has an offtake agreement with Microsoft for 50 MW in 2028 and an agreement with Nucor Steel, which invested $35 million, for 500 MW when the project is complete, Nucor reported.

Other companies without offtake agreements are still working toward launching their technologies.

General Fusion and TAE Technologies are the only publicly traded fusion companies. General Fusion says it expects its magnetized target fusion machine to produce net energy gain in 2035, the industry report said.

TAE Technologies says its Field-Reversed Configuration linear fusion reactor could achieve net energy gain in a pilot project by 2031.

The challenges

During a Reddit "ask me anything" virtual event last month, Commonwealth scientists said they are working through immediate scaling challenges facing SPARC and ARC. To reduce costs, they also need to optimize their supply chain and development ecosystem, find the most efficient tokamak materials, and fine-tune the shape of the plasma, they said.

EPRI's Sowder sees another critical issue.

In Commonwealth's tokamak, tritium released at high temperature by collisions of neutrons with lithium can escape the plasma, he said. It requires a "blanket" to capture and use the heat from the escaping particles to generate electricity by conventional methods, he added.

"Blanket technology is immature globally," but "it can be done at scale," Sowder said. It is a critical piece of the fusion puzzle because "extraneous" tritium can create handling and other difficulties, he added.

Jon Hillesheim, Commonwealth's principal scientist, acknowledged the challenge during the Reddit event.

The "blanket must be thick enough to capture energy from the high-speed neutrons that fusion produces and to shield the rest of the machine from them."

On July 1, Commonwealth announced it had joined LIBRTI, the United Kingdom's national fusion laboratory's world-leading program to test new tritium blanket technologies, said Yost.

That will help meet its plan to demonstrate the blanket technology in parallel to completing SPARC so there will be experience and proof of execution before ARC is built, Yost said.

The blanket problem is particular to some types of fusion technology, but, Sowder said, they all face challenges.

"All the approaches to fusion need to be built, demonstrated, and operated to failure," Sowder said. "Until there is actual evidence of net energy gain from a demonstration, none can be called a winner."

AI Analysis

Commonwealth Fusion Systems' $1 billion funding round, bringing total capital to $4 billion, signals substantial institutional confidence in commercial fusion despite decades of skepticism. The $4 billion in backing from pension funds, sovereign wealth funds, and major corporations like Google and ENI demonstrates that fusion energy is transitioning from speculative research to a capital-intensive industrial endeavor. With SPARC demonstration reactor 80% complete and a planned 400-MW commercial plant targeting early 2030s deployment, CFS is pursuing an aggressive timescale that outpaces traditional expert predictions. However, the field remains divided on commercialization viability—while recent breakthroughs like Lawrence Livermore's fusion ignition validate the physics, converting scientific achievement into profitable, grid-scale power generation presents substantial engineering and economic challenges. The influx of institutional capital suggests investors believe CFS can bridge this gap, potentially accelerating the transition from fusion as academic curiosity to operational energy infrastructure.

Corpus Christi seaport latest to partner with MARAD on maritime reactors-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-08-24/article-8332/corpus-christi-seaport-latest-to-partner-with-marad-on-maritime-reactors/

Corpus Christi seaport latest to partner with MARAD on maritime reactors

Published: August 24, 2026

TL;DR

  • The Port of Corpus Christi in Texas signed a memorandum of cooperation with MARAD to explore small modular reactors (SMRs), microgrids, and advanced vessel propulsion systems for maritime applications.
  • Core Power announced a separate partnership with the Port of Corpus Christi to conduct a site-specific maritime nuclear readiness study assessing feasibility of floating nuclear power plants and nuclear-powered commercial ships.
  • This is MARAD's second seaport partnership on maritime reactors, following a similar MOC signed with the Port of Long Beach in California earlier this summer.

Full Article

Federal and port officials sign an MOC to explore maritime nuclear opportunities in the Port of Corpus Christi in Texas. (Photo: MARAD)

The Department of Transportation's Maritime Administration (MARAD) has signed its second memorandum of cooperation with a U.S. seaport to advance the development of small modular reactors to power commercial vessels, ports, and other maritime assets—this time, shifting its attention to the Gulf coast.

The MOC pairs the Port of Corpus Christi in Texas with MARAD to explore SMRs, microgrids, and advanced vessel propulsion systems for maritime applications. Last week, Secretary of Transportation Sean Duffy, MARAD Administrator Stephen Carmel, Port of Corpus Christi CEO Kent Britton, and Port of Corpus Christi Commission Chair Gabe Guerra signed the MOC at a ceremony.

"Small modular reactors have the potential to reshape America's maritime sector, lower shipping costs, and bolster our supply chains," Duffy said in an announcement. "I'm thrilled that the Port of Corpus Christi is embracing this exciting partnership with the Trump administration to ensure the United States leads the way in innovation."

About the Partnership

The MOC will have the parties explore opportunities to advance maritime energy systems, such as through SMR technologies, resilient port microgrids, shoreside power architecture, infrastructure capable of supporting emerging vessel propulsion concepts, and workforce development, according to MARAD.

The Port of Corpus Christi and its customers moved 203.4 million tons of goods in 2025, down 1.5 percent from a record set in 2024. Crude oil shipments hit 127.4 million tons in 2025, while liquefied natural gas exports hit 18.6 million tons. According to the DOT, the Texas seaport is the country's largest port for petroleum exports.

"With this agreement, the Port of Corpus Christi affirms its commitment to work with the administration to promote innovation in the U.S. maritime sector in support of our nation's storied maritime industrial base and the communities around the world that depend upon it," said Jeff Pollack, chief strategy and innovation officer for the Port of Corpus Christi.

Core Power Agreement

In a separate but related announcement, the Port of Corpus Christi and Core Power announced a partnership to conduct a site-specific maritime nuclear readiness study. The study would assess the feasibility of Corpus Christi as a potential location for a floating nuclear power plant for port and regional demand and as a potential gateway for nuclear-powered commercial ships.

"Ports are where energy systems and global trade meet. For floating nuclear power plants, we need to understand sites, grids, customers, operating models and long-term service arrangements. For nuclear-powered commercial ships, we need safe and predictable pathways for transit, berthing and routine port operations," said Core Power CEO Mikal Bøe.

According to Core Power, the study is a preliminary exercise and would not finalize plans for siting, licensing and permitting, construction, investments, or nuclear technology deployment.

MARAD Seaports Relationships

Earlier this summer, MARAD signed an MOC with the Port of Long Beach in southern California. The first-of-its-kind partnership pairs MARAD with the seaport to explore commercial nuclear-powered maritime vessels.

MARAD had issued a request for information in May seeking industry input on the development of domestic, scalable, commercially viable reactors for U.S. marine transportation.

AI Analysis

This partnership represents a strategic expansion of MARAD's maritime nuclear initiative, with the Port of Corpus Christi's significant petroleum export infrastructure positioning it as an ideal testing ground for SMR technology in commercial shipping. The dual-track approach—combining MARAD's regulatory and policy support with Core Power's technical feasibility assessment—demonstrates a pragmatic pathway toward integrating nuclear power into existing port operations. The presence of both government commitment (Secretary Duffy's involvement) and private sector expertise (Core Power) suggests serious momentum behind commercializing maritime nuclear technology, though the preliminary nature of these studies indicates significant technical and regulatory hurdles remain before actual deployment.

Global 2026 draws attention to nuclear fuel developments-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-08-24/article-8333/global-2026-draws-attention-to-nuclear-fuel-developments/

Global 2026 Draws Attention to Nuclear Fuel Developments

Published: August 24, 2026

TL;DR

  • American Nuclear Society's Global 2026 conference highlighted major fuel cycle developments including Urenco's 50% facility expansion, LIS Technologies' laser enrichment site work, and multiple fuel supply agreements
  • Key projects announced include Standard Nuclear's TRISO fuel agreement with Radiant for microreactors, Nano Nuclear's HALEU collaboration with Quadrant Nuclear at Idaho National Laboratory, and GE Vernova-Shine's AI-based spent fuel tracking system
  • Partnerships with national laboratories (Argonne, Case Western) and international expansion efforts (Quantum Leap's uranium conversion in Namibia) demonstrate comprehensive advances across the entire nuclear fuel cycle

Full Article

Last week in Chicago, the American Nuclear Society's Fuel Cycle & Waste Management Division hosted Global 2026, where it was abundantly clear that there are many exciting fuel-related developments in the nuclear power industry. Developments announced last week run the gamut of the fuel cycle, including enrichment, fabrication, and recycling and reprocessing.

Urenco's Facility Expansion

On August 18, Urenco USA held a ground-breaking ceremony for the expansion of its National Enrichment Facility in Eunice, N.M. Secretary of Energy Chris Wright joined Urenco CEO Boris Schucht and other company leaders at the event.

Urenco USA plans to expand capacity at the facility by nearly 50 percent, with a focus on the enhanced production of low-enriched uranium. The company is installing 2.1 million separative work units of new enrichment capacity, based on its gas-centrifuge enrichment technology, in 24 cascades of centrifuges. The first new cascades are scheduled to start production in 2032, with additional cascades to be installed through 2036.

LIST Begins Site Work for LEU-3 Facility

Laser uranium enrichment company LIS Technologies has commenced initial site-preparation work for its 206-acre LIST Island, in the East Tennessee Technology Park Heritage Center of Oak Ridge, the former site of the Manhattan Project–era K-25 gaseous diffusion plant.

Officially called the LEU-3 Facility, LIST Island will serve as the company's commercial laser-based uranium enrichment headquarters and technical campus. It will house the company's Project F.U.E.L. (Future Uranium Enrichment with Lasers) operations and research activities. Initial site work will include comprehensive environmental assessments and site-specific technical feasibility studies, which will inform the subsequent steps of LIS Technologies' $1.38 billion investment to redevelop the area.

Radiant, Standard Nuclear Reach Fuel Agreement

Microreactor developer Radiant has signed a fuel supply agreement with Standard Nuclear for TRISO fuel fabrication to support the planned delivery of Radiant's 1-MW Kaleidos units to government and commercial customers through the early 2030s.

According to the new agreement, Standard Nuclear will supply Radiant with TRISO fuel manufactured to meet specifications for Kaleidos. Radiant stated that the agreement strengthens its "fully owned end-to-end supply chain for Kaleidos production as the company advances full-scale testing, domestic manufacturing and customer deployments."

Nano, Quadrant Make HALEU Agreement

Reactor developer Nano Nuclear Energy and Quadrant Nuclear Industries have signed a memorandum of understanding to establish a nonbinding framework for collaboration on the supply of high-assay low-enriched uranium fuel. According to the MOU, Nano and QNI will engage in discussions regarding the potential supply and long-term offtake of HALEU produced at a facility QNI plans to build at Idaho National Laboratory.

The facility, called Vanguard, is part of QNI's efforts to develop an integrated HALEU production capability in coordination with the Department of Energy and other stakeholders.

The companies stated that their collaboration "reflects a shared commitment to strengthening the domestic nuclear fuel supply chain, accelerating deployment of advanced reactors, including Nano Nuclear's Kronos MMR Energy System, and supporting U.S. energy security and decarbonization objectives."

GE Vernova, Shine Partner on AI-Based Fuel Project

Fusion energy company Shine is partnering with GE Vernova "on a project to develop a modernized and more efficient system to track spent fuel throughout the recycling process," the company said. Led by GE Vernova's Advanced Research Center and funded by the DOE's Advanced Research Projects Agency–Energy (ARPA-E) division, the project will use artificial intelligence to optimize spent nuclear fuel tracking and measurement at recycling facilities.

The partnership will focus on the development of Monochromatic Assays Yielding Enhanced Reliability (MAYER), a technology meant to track and measure nuclear material in real time as it flows through a recycling facility. The gathered data could be fed into a virtual digital twin, which would be used to strengthen security safeguards and reduce operating costs.

Shine-Argonne-Case Western Collaboration

Argonne National Laboratory and Case Western Reserve University are also partnering with Shine Technologies to apply Argonne's Packed Centrifugal Equipment for Radiochemical Separation (PACERS) chemical separation technology to the nuclear fuel recycling process on which the company is working. PACERS can be used in multiple separation processes, including those that separate strontium-90 and americium-241 radioisotopes for medical, manufacturing, and advanced energy purposes.

The collaboration is being funded through ARPA-E's Converting UNF Radioisotopes Into Energy (CURIE) Program and is being led by Case Western Reserve University.

ASP Isotopes Investigates Uranium Conversion

ASP Isotopes subsidiary Quantum Leap Energy has announced that it is evaluating opportunities in Namibia regarding uranium conversion, the process between mining and enrichment in which uranium concentrate is converted to uranium hexafluoride.

Quantum Leap began the evaluation with a public environmental and social impact assessment and a consultation process for a proposed pilot-scale hydrofluorination and fluorination facility in the Walvis Bay area of Namibia, which would be used to develop, test, and demonstrate hydrofluorination and fluorination processes for metals.

ASP Isotopes established Quantum Leap to focus on uranium conversion; enrichment of uranium-235 for production of HALEU, LEU, and LEU+ fuels; isotopic separation of lithium-6 and lithium-7; and radioactive waste treatment technologies.

AI Analysis

Global 2026's fuel cycle announcements reflect a comprehensive push to strengthen U.S. nuclear supply chain independence across all stages—from enrichment expansion at Urenco and innovative laser enrichment at LIS Technologies to advanced fuel fabrication and recycling partnerships. The convergence of government support (DOE funding, Secretary of Energy presence), private sector investment ($1.38B for LIST Island alone), and collaborative research initiatives with national laboratories signals serious commitment to accelerating advanced reactor deployment. These developments address critical infrastructure gaps while positioning the U.S. to compete globally in next-generation nuclear technologies and domestic energy security.

INL’s HFEF celebrates landmark status with plaque unveiling-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-08-24/article-8331/inls-hfef-celebrates-landmark-status-with-plaque-unveiling/

INL's HFEF celebrates landmark status with plaque unveiling

Published: August 24, 2026

TL;DR

  • The Hot Fuel Examination Facility (HFEF) at Idaho National Laboratory was officially designated as a Nuclear Historic Landmark, making it INL's 10th such landmark and one of 101 honored by the American Nuclear Society since 1985.
  • ANS President Mark Peters unveiled the plaque on August 12, highlighting that HFEF has served the nation for over 50 years and continues to play a critical role in America's nuclear resurgence.
  • HFEF operates the largest inert atmosphere hot cell in the U.S. and houses the Neutron Radiography Reactor (NRAD), both essential facilities for postirradiation examination of nuclear fuels and materials.

Full Article

American Nuclear Society President Mark Peters visited Idaho National Laboratory on August 12 to unveil the plaque that officially designates the Hot Fuel Examination Facility (HFEF) a Nuclear Historic Landmark.

Landmark status was conferred on HFEF last October. It is INL's 10th landmark on the list and one of 101 the Society has honored since 1985. ANS's Nuclear Historic Landmark Award memorializes sites and facilities where outstanding physical accomplishments that advanced the peaceful uses of nuclear energy have taken place. The award recognizes facilities that were in service at least 20 years ago.

Also on hand for the ceremony were John Wagner, INL director; Jess Gehin, associate lab director for nuclear science and technology; Fabiola Cappia, fuels postirradiation examinations division director; Eric Papaionnou, environment, safety, health & quality executive director; and other INL leadership and staff.

The crowd gathered outside of HFEF, which is located in INL's Materials and Fuels Complex, to hear remarks and celebrate the achievement. After speeches and the unveiling of the plaque, Peters and a small group toured HFEF to learn more about the current work being done in the facility.

Quotable

Social media posts captured the pride of the moment, which was decades in the making. Peters shared on LinkedIn that the day was particularly meaningful for him, as a past director of INL, because the lab played such an important role in his professional journey.

"HFEF is more than a remarkable facility—it represents generations of scientists, engineers, operators, technicians, and support teams who have built and sustained a capability that has served the nation for more than half a century."

Wagner shared similar sentiments:

"HFEF was built by people who had the foresight to design not only for the mission in front of them, but for missions they couldn't yet imagine. . . . Today, we're living that future mission. My predecessor and current ANS president said it best: 'It takes generations of work to get to a summer like this.'"

Some Background

In his remarks at the celebration, Wagner highlighted the decades of researchers, engineers, technicians, and operators who have worked at HFEF and moved its mission forward, and how the facility is playing an important role in America's nuclear resurgence.

Established in 1975, HFEF houses the largest inert atmosphere hot cell in the United States for the postirradiation examination of nuclear fuels and materials. It is also home to the Neutron Radiography Reactor (NRAD), a TRIGA reactor in operation since 1977 that produces detailed radiographic images of irradiated fuels and materials. Most recently, NRAD was used to perform zero-power criticality testing of Deployable Energy's Unity demonstration reactor to validate its core physics.

According to Douglas Crawford, chief reactors technologist at the Materials and Fuels Complex:

"In its second half-century, HFEF's workload is as heavy as it's ever been. HFEF's flexibility and continued adaptation to changing needs over 50 years is remarkable."

AI Analysis

This article celebrates an important milestone in nuclear facility history, marking HFEF's recognition as a Nuclear Historic Landmark—a testament to over 50 years of continuous operation and scientific contribution. The facility's designation underscores the broader narrative of America's nuclear resurgence, with HFEF remaining at the forefront of advanced fuel examination and materials research. The emphasis on generational effort and forward-thinking facility design reflects how scientific infrastructure investments yield benefits far beyond their original conceived mission, serving evolving national priorities and emerging energy technologies.

Niger has nationalized-Mining.com

Source: https://www.mining.com/web/niger-awards-former-orano-operated-uranium-mine-to-state-company/

Niger awards former Orano-operated uranium mine to state company

Published: August 23, 2026

TL;DR

  • Niger granted a uranium-mining permit for the In Azaoua area previously held by French company Orano to the state-controlled Tsumco SA following the nationalization of the Somaïr mine
  • This move reflects the military junta's increasingly assertive control over Niger's uranium industry since seizing power three years ago
  • Orano has opposed the nationalization and pursued legal action in multiple commercial courts

Main Article

External view of the Somair plant. (Image: Orano)

Niger granted a large-scale uranium-mining permit previously held by French nuclear company Orano SA to a state-controlled entity, cementing the military junta's takeover of the Somaïr mine.

The permit for the In Azaoua area was granted to Tsumco SA, which Niger created after nationalizing Somaïr, minutes from a Nigerien cabinet meeting on Friday show. Orano previously held 63% of Somaïr, which operated the mine in partnership with Sopamin, Niger's state mining company.

Orano didn't respond to requests for comment outside normal business hours.

The decision underscores Niger's increasingly assertive approach to its uranium industry since soldiers seized power in the West African nation three years ago. It allows mining of the nuclear fuel to continue under the new Nigerien-controlled structure and marks another step in the military government's push to increase state control of the industry.

Niger seized control of Somaïr in June 2025, after accusing Orano of extracting more uranium than its agreement with the state allowed. Orano has opposed the nationalization of the mine and sued Niger in multiple commercial courts following the takeover.

Additional Regulatory Action

In a separate decision on Friday, Niger reawarded the Madaouela I uranium permit to Madaouela Mining Co., with the company agreeing to pay the state an initial fixed fee of $10 million.

The permit had reverted to the state after Niger revoked Canadian explorer GoviEx Uranium's licence in July 2024. GoviEx subsequently launched arbitration proceedings over the cancellation.

By Katarina Höije

AI Analysis

Niger's decision to transfer the Somaïr uranium permit to state-controlled Tsumco SA represents a significant shift in the nation's mineral governance strategy under military leadership. The move follows a pattern of resource nationalization seen across several African nations seeking greater control and revenue from strategic commodities like uranium. As global uranium demand remains elevated due to nuclear energy expansion, control over production capacity carries substantial geopolitical and economic significance, particularly for a nation in West Africa. The ongoing legal disputes with Orano highlight the complexities and tensions that arise when governments pursue nationalization policies against established foreign operators. This development may influence foreign investment sentiment in Niger's mining sector and could set precedents for how other nations manage relationships with international mining companies.

NuScale Power-NuScale

NuScale Power Deploys Nuclear-Specific AI from NPX and Nuclearn to Speed Development of Its Advanced Reactor Program

Published: August 25, 2026

TL;DR

  • NuScale Power is deploying nuclear-specific AI tools from Nuclearn and NPX to accelerate engineering and knowledge management for its small modular reactor (SMR) program
  • Proof-of-concept testing showed the AI reduced information retrieval time by up to 80% while maintaining safety and regulatory compliance standards
  • The partnership combines NuScale's reactor expertise, Nuclearn's AtomAssist platform, and NPX's nuclear project implementation experience to create purpose-built tools for the nuclear industry

Main Article

CORVALLIS, Ore., PHOENIX, Ariz., and KINCARDINE, Ont., Canada — NuScale Power Corporation (NYSE: SMR) (NuScale), the industry-leading provider of proprietary and innovative advanced small modular reactor (SMR) nuclear technology, today announced it is deploying nuclear-specific AI tools to support its engineering and knowledge-management functions as it advances the next generation of nuclear energy. The initiative combines NuScale's advanced reactor expertise, Nuclearn's robust AtomAssist nuclear AI platform, and NPX's nuclear project and AI implementation experience to create purpose-built AI tools that will help NuScale to find, understand, and apply critical information more efficiently as it advances its groundbreaking SMR technology.

As advanced reactor projects move from design into deployment, a key task is making timely, informed decisions across a growing volume of engineering information, licensing evidence, technical standards, and institutional knowledge. Rapid, traceable retrieval of information is essential to maintaining program schedules. NuScale's initial proof of concept demonstrated that designed-for-nuclear AI terminology and workflows could reduce the time required to find relevant information by as much as 80% while surfacing the most relevant engineering methods and standards.

"NuScale is building a transformative deployment model to integrate NuScale Power Modules™ (NPMs) into nuclear power plants in the near-term, which requires executing with speed, precision, and confidence," said Dr. José Reyes, Chief Technology Officer of NuScale Power. "This partnership with Nuclearn and NPX will equip our teams with the right purpose-built AI tools – grounded in our extensive proprietary knowledge –to work more efficiently while maintaining the highest safety and quality standards."
"AI is only as valuable as the knowledge it can be trusted to deliver," said Jerrold Vincent, CFO and Co-Founder of Nuclearn. "In a highly regulated, safety-critical industry like nuclear, every answer must be grounded in controlled engineering and licensing information. Our platform understands the operational context, regulatory implications, and safety considerations behind every decision, and gives engineers rapid access to the knowledge they need. By combining nuclear-specific language models with NuScale's trusted engineering and licensing content, we're helping NuScale maintain complete traceability back to the original controlled source, enabling the team to move faster without reducing safety, quality, or regulatory compliances."
"Across Canada, the United States, and the global nuclear market, new nuclear projects face the same challenge: how to move faster without compromising quality, safety, or regulatory discipline," said Joshua Bell, Director, NPX US. "NuScale is taking a practical step the rest of the industry can learn from. NPX brings the nuclear project experience and implementation discipline needed to turn promising technology into practical tools that teams can use in their day-to-day work. This is not AI for AI's sake. It is nuclear professionals working together to solve nuclear challenges using new technology, grounded in deep experience and the discipline our industry requires."

About NuScale Power

Founded in 2007, NuScale Power Corporation (NYSE: SMR) is the industry-leading provider of proprietary and innovative advanced small modular reactor (SMR) nuclear technology, with a mission to help power the global energy transition by delivering safe, scalable, and reliable carbon-free energy. The NuScale Power Module™, the company's groundbreaking SMR technology, is a small, safe, pressurized water reactor that can each generate 77 megawatts of electricity (MWe) or 250 megawatts thermal (gross), and can be scaled to meet customer needs through an array of flexible configurations up to 924 MWe (12 modules) of output.

As the first and only SMR to have its designs certified by the U.S. Nuclear Regulatory Commission, NuScale is well-positioned to serve diverse customers across the world by supplying nuclear energy for electrical generation, data centers, district heating, desalination, commercial-scale hydrogen production, and other process heat applications.

About NPX

NPX helps nuclear organizations accelerate the path from strategy to delivery. With experience in new nuclear project management, technology selection, engineering and licensing support, vendor coordination, and transformation, NPX works with utilities, vendors, technology developers, and project teams across Canada, the United States, and international markets. By combining nuclear project expertise with practical AI and digital implementation, NPX helps organizations turn fragmented project information into actionable intelligence, strengthen execution discipline, and move complex nuclear initiatives forward with greater speed and confidence. For more information, visit www.npxinnovation.ca.

About Nuclearn

Nuclearn is a leading AI platform purpose-built for nuclear professionals and critical infrastructure operators. Founded by nuclear industry veterans, Nuclearn delivers secure, on-premise-ready AI solutions that help organizations modernize document-intensive workflows, improve efficiency, and preserve institutional knowledge while supporting the rigorous safety, quality, and regulatory standards of the nuclear industry. For more information, visit www.nuclearn.ai.

All forward-looking statements in this press release are based on information available to each of us as of the date hereof, and neither of us assumes any obligation to update the forward-looking statements provided to reflect events that occur or circumstances that exist after the date on which they were made.

AI Analysis

This announcement demonstrates a significant strategic shift in the nuclear industry toward leveraging specialized AI for regulatory compliance and knowledge management—areas where precision and traceability are non-negotiable. The 80% reduction in information retrieval time during the proof-of-concept phase signals genuine operational value beyond hype, addressing a real bottleneck in nuclear project execution where regulatory deadlines and safety verification are critical. By building AI systems specifically trained on nuclear engineering standards and licensing frameworks rather than general-purpose models, NuScale is establishing a template for how regulated industries can adopt AI safely: domain-specific models grounded in controlled knowledge sources rather than broad web data. This partnership between reactor developers, AI specialists, and project consultants reflects industry maturity in understanding that AI deployment requires both technical innovation and deep sector expertise. The approach has potential to accelerate not just NuScale's programs but to become a reference model for how emerging nuclear projects worldwide can compress timelines without sacrificing the rigorous quality standards the industry demands.

Aalo Atomics achieves criticality on July 4-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-07-06/article-8182/aalo-atomics-achieves-criticality-on-july-4/

Aalo Atomics achieves criticality on July 4

Published: July 6, 2026

TL;DR

  • Aalo Atomics achieved criticality on July 4, 2026, at 12:20 a.m. MT at Idaho National Laboratory with a low-enriched uranium–fueled, sodium-cooled test reactor, becoming the fourth DOE-authorized reactor to hit this milestone.
  • The criticality milestone was accomplished in just eight months from breaking ground, making it one of the fastest reactor builds in modern American history.
  • Aalo plans to expand into commercial production with Project Ascension, aiming to produce electricity and power an on-site data center in 2027, with ultimate goals of mass-producing modular "Aalo Pod" nuclear plants for AI data centers.

Full Article

Executive Order 14301 set an ambitious goal for at least three test reactors to achieve criticality by July 4. Two private companies participating in the Department of Energy's Reactor Pilot Program—Antares and Valar Atomics—reached this stage earlier in June, and Deployable Energy—participating in the DOE's Nuclear Energy Launch Pad—became the third last week.

In the last few weeks, reports indicated that Aalo would be next, reaching criticality at Idaho National Laboratory with a low-enriched uranium–fueled, sodium-cooled reactor on or near the target date set forth by President Trump's EO 14301. In the early hours of July 4, Aalo's critical test reactor—a full-scale zero-power version of its planned 10-MWe Aalo-X—did just that, becoming the fourth DOE-authorized reactor to hit the milestone.

"We are proud to play a major role in America's nuclear renaissance, going from breaking ground to a sustained chain reaction in just eight months, one of the fastest reactor builds in modern American history," said Yasir Arafat, president and chief technical officer of Aalo Atomics, in the company's announcement of its achievement.
"This is exactly what a nuclear renaissance looks like: American companies, moving at American speed, proving novel reactor designs real one after another," INL Director John Wagner said in a news release congratulating the Aalo team.

About Aalo's Criticality

According to Austin, Texas–based Aalo, criticality was achieved on Saturday at 12:20 a.m. MT. The test reactor includes a full-scale core, intended to demonstrate the nuclear components of a 10-MWe reactor. Aalo's ultimate objective is to include multiple full-scale power reactors in a 50-MWe commercial "Aalo Pod" to power AI data centers.

"We took on the largest scope within the Reactor Pilot Program: We built, licensed, and operated the reactor facility from scratch. We stood up our own safety programs, built 10 MW worth of fuel assemblies, and manufactured and quality-controlled commercial-scale systems in-house," the company said of its achievement.

Work is already underway on a second reactor for Aalo's Project Ascension. This endeavor will house a commercial-scale 10-MWe reactor at Aalo's INL site that will produce electricity and power an on-site data center in 2027.

"Criticality has validated our supply chain, reactor physics, control systems, and fueling procedures at commercial scale. We are now expanding into a one-million square-foot factory to apply assembly-line manufacturing to reactor production, which will open the door to mass-producing the Aalo Pod, our fully modular nuclear plant purpose-built for AI data centers," the company said.

A Whirlwind 2026

In March, Aalo announced that it had signed fuel fabrication contracts with Global Nuclear Fuel. That same month, Aalo said that Urenco USA had completed enriching the uranium hexafluoride feedstock needed for its fuel and had delivered it to GNF for fuel rod fabrication. The company also announced it had selected Baker Hughes for a 10-MWe steam turbine generator set and associated ancillary systems.

And the Others

Four DOE-authorized test reactors have now hit criticality. But what of the other reactor participants in the Reactor Pilot Program and the Nuclear Energy Launch Pad? Nuclear News provided an update on the statuses of their respective reactor technologies last week.

AI Analysis

Aalo Atomics' achievement represents a significant milestone in the advanced reactor development landscape, validating the commercial viability of small modular reactors fueled by low-enriched uranium. The eight-month timeline from groundbreaking to criticality demonstrates that American private industry can move with remarkable speed on nuclear projects when given clear government support and streamlined regulatory pathways. This success, coupled with the three other DOE-authorized reactors reaching criticality, signals that the "nuclear renaissance" narrative is transitioning from aspirational rhetoric to demonstrated technical and commercial reality, with data center power demands potentially serving as the economic engine driving deployment of these novel reactor designs.

Air Force selects three microreactor developers for ANPI-Nuclear Newswire

Source: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-04-23/article-7972/air-force-selects-three-microreactor-developers-for-anpi/

Air Force selects three microreactor developers for ANPI

April 23, 2026

TL;DR

  • The Air Force selected three companies—Radiant Industries, Antares Nuclear, and Westinghouse Government Services—to develop microreactors for military installations under the Advanced Nuclear Power for Installations (ANPI) program.
  • Each company is paired with a specific Air Force base: Radiant at Buckley Space Force Base (Colorado), Antares at Joint Base San Antonio (Texas), and Westinghouse at Malmstrom Air Force Base (Montana).
  • The ANPI program aims to have at least one 3-10 MWe microreactor operational at a military installation by 2030, with formal siting and environmental analyses now proceeding.

---

Overview

The Department of the Air Force has selected three companies to be potential developers and operators of microreactors at Air Force installations as part of the Advanced Nuclear Power for Installations (ANPI) program: Radiant Industries, Antares Nuclear, and Westinghouse Government Services.

The ANPI program is a collaboration among the Air Force, Army, and Defense Innovation Unit that has a demo target date of 2030 for at least one 3 MWe–10 MWe microreactor on at least one military installation.

The Partnerships

According to the Department of the Air Force, each company was paired with a particular installation in order to maximize energy resilience and align mission requirements with site-specific characteristics.

Radiant Industries was chosen to develop a microreactor tailored to the needs of Buckley Space Force Base in Colorado. Antares Nuclear was picked to build a microreactor for Joint Base San Antonio in Texas. Westinghouse Government Services, part of Westinghouse Electric Company, was selected for the microreactor project at Malmstrom Air Force Base in Montana.

With the companies matched to the three military sites, two of which were announced as potential locations for microreactors earlier in April, formal siting and environmental analyses can now proceed. Those analyses will follow processes required by the National Environmental Policy Act.

"The future of air and space dominance is powered by resilient energy. By integrating advanced nuclear technology, we are not just keeping the lights on; we are guaranteeing that our most critical national security missions will never be held at risk by a power outage. This is a pivotal moment for the Department of the Air Force."

— Michael Borders, Assistant Secretary of the Air Force for Energy, Installations and Environment

Radiant and Buckley

Radiant Industries is in the process of developing the Kaleidos high-temperature, gas-cooled microreactor. According to Chief Revenue Officer Mike Starrett:

"The American energy industry needs to make progress now, not in 5 or 10 years. With a full-power reactor already under assembly, Radiant will deliver."

Col. Eamon Murray, the Space Base Delta 2 commander at Buckley Space Force Base, Radiant's designated partner, said:

"A secure and reliable power source will enable Buckley's warfighters from all six services and allied nations to support the Joint Force in all situations, and we are proud to lead the charge to advance our warfighters' technological superiority. The success of this endeavor depends on strong community trust we've built through proactive engagement and communication, and we're excited to continue that engagement and transparency throughout this process."

Antares and Joint Base San Antonio

Antares Nuclear is currently developing its R1 sodium heat pipe–cooled microreactor. CEO Jordan Bramble said:

"We are grateful and proud to partner with Joint Base San Antonio, the Department of the Air Force, and the Defense Innovation Unit. We built this company to deliver resilient power for missions like this."

Brig. Gen. Randy Oakland, commander of the Joint Base and of the 502d Air Base Wing, said that energy resilience:

"is imperative to sustaining operations. If selected as a site under this initiative, Joint Base San Antonio's resilience would take a tangible step forward to ensure reliable support for its many important missions."

Westinghouse and Malmstrom

Westinghouse's eVinci microreactor has been in development for several years and has been approved for testing at the National Reactor Innovation Center's Demonstration of Microreactor Experiments (DOME) test bed at Idaho National Laboratory.

Rich Rademacher, president of Westinghouse Government Services, said:

"eVinci is perfectly suited to ensure our armed forces have the reliable and resilient energy they need for mission-critical operations globally. We look forward to continuing our strong partnership with the Department of the Air Force and the Defense Innovation Unit."

Other Military Microreactors

In addition to the ANPI program, the Air Force is planning to demonstrate the feasibility and operational benefits of a microreactor at Eielson Air Force Base in Alaska. Furthermore, the Army's Janus Program has the goal of deploying a demonstration microreactor at a military installation by 2030.

---

AI Analysis

This announcement represents a significant milestone in U.S. military nuclear modernization, establishing clear partnerships and timelines for microreactor deployment across strategic installations. The selection of three distinct developers—each with different reactor designs (Radiant's gas-cooled, Antares's sodium heat pipe-cooled, and Westinghouse's proven eVinci)—allows the military to evaluate multiple technologies and risk profiles simultaneously. The 2030 demonstration target with environmental review processes provides a realistic timeline while the base-specific pairing strategy suggests thoughtful consideration of operational requirements and community engagement. This multi-pronged approach positions the Pentagon to overcome historical nuclear project delays while maintaining environmental and regulatory compliance.
